Said to be the bastard young of Nidhogg, the serpent at the root of Yggdrasil, viper trees are white, scaly trees with living snakes' heads as their branches. From a distance they appear as white beeches or similar trees, but viewed up close they have clearly reptilian skin and features. Though they can writhe and reach as snakes do, usually viper trees simply sway in the breeze as other trees — but they also move even in the absence of any breeze.

Viper trees speak the language of tanar'ri and no other. Groves of viper trees hiss and whisper to each other unnervingly during the night, speaking of their kills, their hungers, and their treasures. They are common in Azzagrat, the 45th to 47th layers of the Abyss; elsewhere in the Abyss they are used as guards in gardens, around moats, and at gates.

**COMBAT:** Single viper trees rarely attack creatures larger than size S. Viper tree groves (such as the Viper Forest of Zrintor) are notably more aggressive, willing to attack small groups of size M creatures if the group is perceived as sufficiently weak. They can swallow even size L creatures, if given enough time.

A viper tree has dozens of serpentlike heads and branches, but the tree can only command a few of them at a time. When a branch is slain, one of the tree's "sleeping" branches wakes, for the brain of a viper tree is actually deep in the tree's heartwood. As a result viper trees get their full complement of attacks until they are near death. The bite of a viper tree inflicts 2d6 points of damage.

Viper tree venom is insidious and potent; anyone bitten by a viper tree must make a saving throw against poison at -3. Victims that fail lose 4 points of Dexterity permanently and are immobilized by the venom for 48 hours, long enough for the tree to swallow even the largest prey. The venom has an onset time of 2d4 rounds. Even if the saving throw succeeds, the victim temporarily loses 4 points of Dexterity as a result of the shakes and trembling the venom induces for the next 48 hours. Neutralize poison removes the Dexterity loss immediately, but does nothing for the paralysis. It even prevents the permanent Dexterity loss if applied within an hour. *Remove paralysis* cures the twitching and immobility, but does nothing for the Dexterity loss. Viper trees are immune to their own venom.

Because of their multiple heads, viper trees are unaffected by most spells that target a single or a few creatures, such as *charm monster*, *hold person*, or *sleep*. To affect a viper tree, such a spell must affect a number of creatures equal to the viper tree's Hit Dice.

Viper trees are immune to cold, venom, and acid attacks, and they take half damage from blunt weapons and normal damage from electrical attacks. Their woody stumps bleed a brownish-amber sap when cut, and the wood burns quickly. Viper trees suffer double damage from fire.

If attacked with missile weapons, viper trees can break off their own branches to crawl toward their attackers. These branches ooze sap from their broken end and die within an hour, so the trees are reluctant to lose them, but the broken branches have the same hit points, THAC0, and damage as the parent tree.

**HABITAT/SOCIETY:** Viper trees are a strange hybrid of tanar'ri, reptile, and plant, a sort of fiendish, egg-laying plant. They lay eggs once a month, and each egg lies protected at the base of the its parent. Once it hatches on its own, the newly hatched viper tree is abandoned by its parent. The young go through a mobile stage before rooting in the body of their first large prey.

Great groves of viper trees grow on the sites of some Blood War battlefields, where the trees defend themselves against attacks from baatezu by growing in large clusters. The viper trees allow tanar'ri armies to pass through freely and even take cover under their branches, but baatezu are always attacked, even if all the viper trees are slain as a result. Yugoloth armies are usually ignored.

A legend exists among the tanar'ri that the lords of Baator once amused themselves by forming viper trees from manes and other creatures that they captured in the Blood War. Others say that the Abyssal lords made examples of a thousand least tanar'ri who refused to march against a position that a million of their fellows had already failed to take. In either case, they were once tanar'ri, and this is why they usually side with the tanar'ri against the baatezu. The tanar'ri still tell the tale to prevent desertions, but it may hold a kernel of truth to it: Some baatezu lords are believed to still know the secret to the transformation.

**ECOLOGY:** According to a Harmonium poll designed to discover ideal carnivorous plant preferences (involving 47 viper trees

30

![Illustration of a viper tree, a plant-like creature with numerous long, snake-headed branches twisting upwards.](page_246_image_1_v2.jpg)

in three separate groves), the tastiest forms of prey are "small, easy to swallow, like squirrels — they tickle bark," followed by anything else seeking shelter ("they're so funny when they're scared"), eyewings ("gooey"), and last by small flying creatures that mistakenly nest in their branches ("too many feathers, except bats"). Large viper trees also eat "walkers" — dretch, lemures, manes, nupperibo, rutterkin, and other weak, marginally moronic deserters who haunt the battlefields of the Blood War in droves — but they claim that "walkers sting, and taste like fear." In lean times, 4 out of 5 viper trees claim that they can survive on a form of magical sustenance ("eat spells"), the waters of the Styx ("forgot what it tastes like, but so sleepy"), and the nutrients in the blood-soaked battlefields of the Blood War ("red dirt is good"). Only 1 in 10 viper trees was able to overwhelm a Harmonium questioner.

## LARVAL FORM

In their larval form, viper trees resemble fully mobile, three-headed snakes. Larval trees have only 2 Hit Dice, and only two of the heads of the newly hatched creature are fully active — the third is a sort of runt. The tiny, inactive head is always the central one, which is carried along by the other two until it awakens after a period of about one month. Thereafter the third head is the directing intelligence of the entire creature, and it begins searching for a suitable place to put down roots. When the larval tree kills suitably large prey, it lodges its tail through the kill and into the earth and begins the growth of its plant phase.

The larval viper tree is insatiably hungry, constantly devouring manes, cranium rats, and other small prey. It can strike a single target with two heads, while the third protects it against attacks from any other direction. Its venom immobilizes prey by inducing twitching spasms that last for 1d10 hours (a saving throw versus poison at T2 reduces this to a 2-point Dexterity loss, which fades within a day).

# AN ABRIDGED LEXICON OF THE ABYSS

AN ACCOUNT OF A NUMBER OF THE LESS WELL KNOWN AND TRAVELED LAYERS OF THE INFINITE PLANE, FOR THE EDIFICATION DF THE INQUISITIVE AND THE SUICIDAL. EXCERPTED WITH THE KIND PERMISSION OF THE FRATERNITY OF ORDER (ALSO CALLED THE GUVNERS).

By Appointment to Her Fearsome, Regal, and Unstoppable Majesty, the Lady of Pain, by Alonthas the Dark, scrivener and bookbinder to the Court.

The plane called the Abyss is named for its fourth layer, though that layer was discovered after the Plain of Infinite Portals. Most of its layers are accessible to any fool who cares to venture into them, though many have wisely been proscribed by the Interdict of our factol. Though they are listed here, they are named only to warn the wise. Barmies who ignore the Interdicts don't risk the wrath of the factol; the tanar'ri and deadly conditions of those layers are punishment enough.

A few layers are known only by reputation, their entrances on the Plain of Infinite Portals long since sealed. These are called the Lost Planes of the Abyss, sealed zones where each summoned gate only loops back into itself. Many Abyssal lords dread finding themselves in one of these pocket planes, helpless until someone releases them. Many other tanar'ri lords, of course, are struggling and paying a bloody price to find out the dark of how to put their enemies there. The cross-trade in rumors iand half-truths is a quick one. 
Realms and layers are often one and the siame in the Abyss, as each power and even the lesser Abyssal lords do not tolerate interlopers on their layer; though the struggles may take aeons, the result is always the complete submergence of the entire layer into a single realm. Several well-known open (independent) layers answer to no one because no lord or power will permit a single ruler; the most famous of these is the Plain of Infinite Portals. A similarly crucial point is the gateway layer of Durao, a major debarkation point for tanar'ri on their way to fight the Blood War.

The realms are listed in numerical order below. Each number refers to the order in which the layer was discovered and recorded in the Lexicon — no relationship between the: layers themselves is clear. Of course, most of the discoveries were unpleasant ones. The current count is 679 layers, 141 of them habit:able by most planars with little or no magical assistance. Direct portals from Sigil to the Abyss only reach the Plain of Infinite Portals and a limited number of the other layers.

**LAYER THE FiRST**, the Plain of Infinite Portals, discovered in the fourth year of the reign of Clarille the Munificent, factol of the Fraternity of Order. Three travelers - the Archmage Unther, Sir Carolan the Lord-Provost of the Seventh Tower, and an unnamed minstrel - stumbled through a previously unknown gate and discovered the long-hidden home of the fearsome race known as the tanar'ri. Though the minstrel did not survive his discovery, both Unther and Sir Carolan returned to the City Courts and gave this account of what they saw:

> Beyond the Gate we found ourselves on a great Plain pitted with craters, and defended by fortresses of Iron wherein dwelt the cruel Tanar'ri. The first we saw of the Tanar'ri was a swirling Mob that o'erran us and slew our minstrel. though we gave them no cause for strife. A Tanar'ri lord gave us shelter in the citadel of Broken Reach in return for the carcass of our minstrel, who was barely recognizable in any event. After speaking to the creatures of the fortress, we learned that each Pit of the Plain leads to another Layer, and the number of Pits seems great indeed. Despite the Horrors of the Plain itself, surely great treasures and wonders lie beyond these Gates, sights of glory that no guesting heart can live without. We shall return as soon as we report to the right honorable Factol.

Groups are even now being readied to venture into the Pit-Gates and discover what other realms lie beyond.

**Layer The Second**, called the "Driller's Hives," discovered in the fifth year of the reign of Clarille, factol of the Fraternity of Order, by the good and loyal followers of the faction now resting in the Fraternity's hospice. Only the Lord-Provost's group has returned from its journey, at half its strength and half-mad with tales of a plane of gargantuan insect hives eternally at war with one another. The bodies of the returning members are riddled with wounds drilled deep into their flesh by these insect creatures. The air of the plane of the Hives transformed the flesh of many of Carolan's company into insect creatures themselves, though paladins seemed immune. Sir Carolan has accepted a commission to oversee the Order's affairs in Mechanus.

**LAyer THe ThirD**, called the Forgotten Land, discovered in the twelfth year of the reign of Clarille, factol of the Fraternity of Order, by a flesh automaton of the Dustmen. According to an automaton newly created by the Dustmen, this layer steals memories, so that most visitors forget where they've come from, where they've been, and what they've seen there. Unliving creatures such as the undead and automatons seem immune.

**LAYEr THE FourTH**, called the Grand Abyss, discovered in the first year of the reign of Factol Jarkopple the Inflexible of the Fraternity of Order. The Grand Abyss is a deep crevasse bridged by and surrounded with narrow paths, where falling stones, darkness, and petrification slew seven of the band of twelve githzerai who discovered it. It is a workplace of the tanar'ri and thcir servants, for they are constantly digging fortifications and shelters out of the walls and dropping the rubble into the Abyss itself. The githzerai have been paid for their trouble, but have refused further employment.

**LAYER THE FIFTH**, called "Wormblood," discovered in the seventh year of the reign of Factol Jarkopple the Inflexible of the Fraternity of Order. Wormblood is a planc to avoid; it infects all visitors with a form of mummy rot that destroys bodies from within. The party of twenty well armed and magically warded adventurers of the Company of the Gyrfalcon who reported it all died within a year, despite all efforts to restore them. Members of the Order have been banned from visiting it; the increasing number of extremely hazardous layers has led Factol Jarkopple to place many of the layers listed in the Lexicon under an interdict. These are so marked henceforth.

**LAYer The SixTH**, called the "Realm of a Million Eyes" and discovered in the ninth year of the reign of Factol Jarkopple the Inflexible of the Fraternity of Order by the mind flayer Illionth, through powers of the mind. According to the visions of Illionth, a respected scholar of the Order these eleven years, this plane is home to the Great Mother, chief goddess of the beholder pantheon. Every form of evil beholder and beholderkin dwells here, preying on one another and any tanar'ri foolish enough to wander into the crossfire. Illionth reported having found the home of "all corruption and foulness" and set forth to lay waste to the plane. He has not yet returned to Sigil to report his success.

**LAYER THE SEvENTH**, called the "Phantom Plane" and discovered by Mordenkainen the Archmage, though it has long been sealed against intruders. Mordenkainen, a prime, spoke to this humble scrivener of his recent travels while commissioning a new traveling spellbook. Weary of the Blood War and far from the front, the Abyssal lord Sess'inek is one of the few tanar'ri who spends little time concerned with the Blood War. The gates to this plane, more often called Kearackinin by the tanar'ri themselves, are all sealed: An extraordinary effort would be required merely to release the locks. Sess'inek is worshiped by lizard men, and his realm is rife with lizard kings, his servitors.

**LAYER THE EIGHTH**, called the "Skin-shedder." Discovered by the tiefling Orzograt. Interdicted. [alignment of any creature that dwells]

**LAYER THE NINTH**, called Burningwater, discovered by the marid Omar al-Djibou. Interdicted. The laver of Burningwater is an ocean of acid inhabited by stone-shelled creatures that resemble twisted lobsters, shellfish, and armored fish. The marid is recovering in the Order's hospics. though his hopes of complete recovery are thin. He has asked us to bar members of the Order from visiting the 9th, which suggestion was approved by the factol the following day.

**LayeR THe TenTH**, called "That Hellhole" by its discoverer, Gorbin of the Clan of Normarrin of the Iron House. Interdicted. The dwarf Gorbin survived this layer entirely alone, though he stayed within its feeding frenzy for only three minutes - the shortest discovery yet for the Lexicon. It seems as if the plane's only food is other sentient creatures, for Gorbin was set up as soon as he clleared the gate. The tooth marks and punctures of his adamantine armor support his story.

**LAYeR The ElEvEnTH**, called "Molrat." Interdicted. A few tunnel-fighting bloods have returned from Molrat, a dark, dank layer of eternal darkness where tanar'ri hunt one another by sense of smell. The creatures fear light and fire (though they can extinguish them through magical means), but the numbers are too large to justify a return trip.

**LayeR The TwelFTH**, also called Twelvetrees. Inadvertently discovered by the aasimon, Twelvetrees has been avoided by travelers ever since. The tanar'ri lords captured twelve devas and brought them here to be sacrificed. It is haunted by their tortured cries, which make conversation impossible. Rurmor says that Twelvetrees is the site of much diabolical magic-working.

**LAyeR THE ThirteenTH**, called the Blood Tor. reported by the cornugon Twist, also called the Foul. According to Twist, the Blood Tor was the site of a baatezu incursion into the Abyss, one of their greatest successes early in the Blood War. The 13th layer is the realm of Beshaba, a power of misfortune. Expeditions there are certain to be haunted by costly mistakes and bad luck; further research to uricover the mystical origins of such misfortunes would be. A call for volunteers among the Fraternity has been posted, without success.

**Layer the Twenty-Third**, called the Iron Wastes or Kostchtchie's Realm. A bitterly cold plane of ice miles deep. the Iron Wastes are the domain of frost giants who serve Kostchtchie, their tanar'ri lord. Devoid of most life, this laver is illuminated by a single distant light, no brighter than a moon. The best shelter is the Glacier Citadel, an ice fortress carved into the moving ice between two towering peaks.

**LayeRS THe FORTy-FiFTH, -SIXTH, AND -SEVENTH**, called Azzagrat or the Triple Realm, discovered by merchants from Sigil. A confusing set of intertwining layers, all under the dominion of the Abyssal lord Graz'zt. The lord made welcome the merchants, inviting them to bring their wares for trade and even providing them with a tanar'ri escort to ensure their safety.

All good brethren of the Fraternity of Order mourn the passing of Alonthas the Dark, bookbinder and scrivener. The honor of compiling the Lexicon has passed to Orrota the Lich, whose steady hand and keen eye have won her the title of Scribe Extraordinary and Arcane to the Oorder.

**Layer the Sixty-sixth**, Lolth's Web, also called the Demonweb Pits, as reported by Grellaen, renegade priestess of Lolth. This convoluted plane connects the places where the Spider-Queen has worshipers, and the folded plane itself resembles a great web. Its few strandlike paths wind through infinite mist filled with howling souls, but each strand loops in a circle that somehow passes both over and under each of the others, though it always seems level. Each strand is strung with gates into the planes where Lolth is strongest, as a string is strung with pearls.

**LAYER THE SIXTY-SEvenTH**, called the Heaving Hills, discovered by the Company of the Phoenix, in the twenty-fourth year of the reign of Factol Zander the Explorer. Struck by constant earthquakes, this swampy layer is half hills and half marshy wastes - and the two terrains switch places with every quake. Strange froghemothlike creatures are adept at surviving these transformations, as are the hezrou who dominate the layer. The guakes often reveal strange ruins said to be the lost cities of the varrangoin.

**LAYER THE SIXTY-EIgHTH**, Unnamed, discovered in the twenty-fourth year of the reign of Factol Zander the Explorer. Interdicted. This barren and abandoned layer is entirely airless. Morgenta the Mage survived due to the protective influence of her ioun stone, though she was forced to watch her companions' blood boil away before her.

**LAYER THE SIXTY-NiNTH**, the Crushing Plain. Interdicted. The landscape of this layer is unknown, for it is teeming with so many chasme and manes and lemures that the fiends must constantly crawl over one another for fear of suffocating beneath the crushing hordes.

**LAYEr THE SeveNTIETH**, called the "Ice Floe." Discovered in the twenty-fifth vear of the reign of Factol Zander. Interdicted. The entire plane is a layer of thin ice over cold black water; any heavy weight. fire, or heat quickly shatters the thin crust of surface ice, sinking the poor berk into the fast currents beneath. The githzerai who discovered the Ice Floe also learned that the layer's cold steals the life energy from any planar, one level each day.

**LAYER THE SevenTY-FiRsT**, called SpiraC, discovered by Haran the Strong in the second year of the reign of Factol Darvik. Haran the Strong lived in Spirac for four years before returning to Sigil, the hub of the planes and home of the marvels of the multiverse. Spirac is a layer of spiky, fernlike trees, and mountains as thin and towering as chimneys in Sigil. The plane is inhabited by manes, hezrou, and true tanar'ri who like the layer's excellent hunting and sport.

**LAYer The Seventy-seconD**, called "Darklight." Discovered by Federo the Venturer in the fourth and last year of the reign of Factol Darvik. Interdicted. In Darklight the shadows of poor berks with good alignments shine like the sun, while evil cutters are wrapped in a fog of darkness. The tanar'ri find this a convenient way to choose victims for slavery or death.

**LAYer The Seventy-THirD**, called the Wells of Darkness and discovered by Ulricon the Necromancer, in the third year of the reign of Soretti the Spider, Regent of the Child-Factol Andalon. This plane is habitable but abandoned by most of the tanar'ri. The stony ground is littered with inky black wells, spots of pure darkness at the bottom of each shaft beneath a steely bluish sun. Each well is said to contain a usurped Abyssal lord, a failed commandet of the Blood War, or a slain true tanar'ri. Each hopes to regain freedom and followers, and some may pledge service to those who visit them. If the tanar'ri have a graveyard, this is it, though no one visits it.

**Layer the Seventy-FourtH**, called the Smaragd, discovered in the second vear of the reign of Factol Soretti the Spider. This realm has ever-shifting colors, a radiant emerald jungle of constant damp heat and large, hunting tribes of fierce petitioners who ferment strange poisons from the diamond-back frogs and the venom glands of tanar'ri. The jungle is also the home of many roving tribes of bar-lgura. Two terrible powers slumbers on the Smaragd: Meershaulk in a snake-infested pit and Ramenos in an enormous hollow tree. Most who intrude on them are simply devoured; the rest survive only because of their all-consuming sloth. Ramenos' tanar'ri followers call him the Sleeping God, and offer sacrifices by placing them within his perpetually open mouth. Other than the serpents, venomous toads, and acidic rains. Smaragd is a sleepy, relatively benign layer of the Abyss.

**LAYER THE EIGHTY-EIGHTH**, called the Gaping Maw, discovered in the tenth year of the reign of Factol Soretti the Spider. A terrible whirlpool in the Styx is supposed to have drawn the first visitors into the Gaping Maw, giving it its name. Fortunately, they were hydroloths, able to quickly find a gate out. The Gaping Maw is a layer of briny water and rocky prominences, most of which are used as rookeries by flying tanar'ri. The depths are ruled by kraken, ixixachitl and their lord Demogorgon, whose stony home is guarded by the most powerful representatives of each of his servant races.

**Layer the One Hundred and Thirteenth**, called Thanatos or the Belly of Death, discovered in the second year of the reign of Factol Masol the Just. Thanatos is a cold, black, nearly airless layer that belongs more to the undead than to the tanar'ri. There is little plant life, few living creatures, and less reason to visit, particularly because the lord that rules here. *at(#$. is rumored to have a foul temper and does not take lightly to intrusions on his domain.

(Historian's note: Recent reports have confirmed that the former lord of this layer is no more, and that Thanatos is now the domain of Kiaransalee, the drow goddess of vengeance and the undead. The new ruler of the layer has declared that the deposed lord's name is never to be spoken, and her decree has apparently reached even to the manuscripts in the Fraternity's libraries; all copies of this manuscript were found to have the former lord's obscured by some unknown means. Our wise factol has determined that, despite the duty of the Fraternity's historians to record the true and accurate chronicle of the planes, we shall not tempt the wrath of a bower by attempting to preserve a name that likely has no further bearing on the continuing history of the Abyss. - Archimndrite Sakij)

**Layer te One HundiAn Eight-fst**, called the Rotting Plain, discovered in the third year of the reig of Factol Jura by a well-organized xpedition from Mechanus. The Rotting Plain i; a pungent expanse of baking, dry savanna and shallow swamplands is the home of Laogzed, power and father othe troglodytes, a gluttonous eater of carrion and souls. Supposedly the off:pring of Panzuriel and a reptilian tanar i mother, the stinking power was quickly banished from Panzuriel's realm. The most common tanar'ri of the plane are masses of glabrezu and rutterlin, with vrock and manes scavengers.

**Layer the Two Hundied and Twenty-sec-oND**, called Shedaklah, more commonly referred to simply asthe Slime Pits. Discovered by heavily potected members of the Doomguard. Interlicted. A bubbling moras of oozing, fetid sludge. Its landscap is little more than the name implies, wth vast expanses of caustic, and unintellgent slime. Strange organic forms are b lieved to rise from the ocean of moldg at the whim of Juiblex, who rules a portion of the layer. The remainder is theprovince of Zuggtmoy, the Abyssal lacy of Fungi, a dread and fell ruler of the Abyss imprisoned somewhere beneath he ground. Legends say that until she is eleased, her realm will continue to slovly rot and crumble. Zuggtmoy's realm i filled with fungi, slimes, molds, ooze, and puddings of every description.

**Layer te Two Hundred and Seventy-FOURTH**, called Durao. Discovered by Oppel and Lara, conscriptsand deserters from the Blood War. Durao is the emtarkation point for tanar'ri hordes on thir way to Gehenna. Its rusting iron whirves and barracks stand between a feid swamp and the sluggish waters ofthe Styx. Day and night, troops march hrough the sprawling iron streets, and the noise of boots on iron rings and reverberates for miles. Marranaeloths are pad a small fortune to pilot enormous troq barges down the river. Other than rusy piers with access to the rest of the Lover Planes, the layer has nothing to recommend it.

**LAYer The Three HundreD AND ThirD**, called the Sulfanorum, discovered by Chumbleburr the Tobacconist, a halfling. Described as the Promised Land by Chumbleburr. the Sulfanorum is in fact tolerable for those who can stand clouds of smoke. The tanar'ri of the layer smoke various pipes full of foul stuff that clogs the air, making planars and pilgrims alike wheeze and ache with each breath, Fires are constantly burning, pouring smoke and filth into the skies. Sulfanorum's fires are weak, though, just enough to light a pipe or start incense smoking. In exchange for his report, Chumbleburr was granted a 50-year monopoly on trade with the layer.

**Layer the Three Hundred and Seventy-sev-ENTH**, called the Plains of Gallenshu and discovered by Xorna, an escaped githyanki slave of the tanar'ri called the armanitcs. This layer is no more than a realm of dust storms and choking grit that scours everything it meets. It is inhabited by terrible bat-winged creatures that battle the skull-helmed tanar'ri that are its greatest slave-takers. Xorna's story keeps returning to "the tnundering nooves," and certain battle-cries and standards. Her report is biased at best.

**Layer the Thee Hunded nd Niety** especially those who make no sacrifices to Urdlen, the gnawing evil gnome power that rules the realm. The inhabitants are Many of the creatures of the Worm Realm suffer from florid fungal and cancerous growths; though it adds color to the realm, long-term visitors to the realm always die of disease. Speculation among the Bargainers is that this disease is the result of a feud between Urdlen and Zuggtmoy. Caution and priestly intervention are advised.

**Layer The Four Hundredth**, called the Woeful Escarand, discovered by the Company of the White Rose in the 21st year of the reign of Factol Karakor the Lawgiver. The Woeful Escarand is one of the few layers of the Abyss that follows a pattern and serves a purpose. Petitioners and new arrivals are herded from the gates over treacherous terrain toward the layer's single visible feature, a great mountain. The gates exiting the plane are all within this central Mountain of Woe, guarded by the nalfeshnee who are the primary occupants of the layer. They sit on mighty thrones of flame, or herd more creatures toward judgment. From atop their thrones, the nalfeshnee cast judgment upon those before them, much more dreadful than the wise and merciful judgment of the Fraternity of Order in Sigil. The discoverers of the layer literally carved their way through the mountain and out again.

**Layer the Four Hundred And Twenty-sec-OND**, called Yeenoghu's Realm. A band of four bariaur bought the tale of this dismal and fetid layer of endless yellow forests and dun savannas, where the plane's Abyssal lord and his retinue hunt the lesser tanar'ri and any visitors. The grasses are edged as sharp as daggers, thornslingers and strangleweed are common, and the water is unclean. Food and air are tolerable. The bariaur all escaped due to their woodcraft and stealth.

**Layer the Five Hundred and Third**, called Torremor or the Layer of Flies. A layer of flying horrors who have no mercy for any creature that cannot similarly glide through the filthy air of the layer. The Abyssal lord of this layer, Pazrael, is often gone from his realm, yet manages to hold on to his domain through cunning and ruthlessness.

**Layer the Five Hundred and Eighty-sixth**, called the Prison of the Mad God. This layer is the prison of Diinkarazan, the mad god of the duergar. It is a swirling vortex of air and gas, with rings of whirling rocks flying about a central point. Diinkarazan is magically bound to a stone throne at the center of the storm. The plane constantly distorts space from minute to minute.

**Layer the Six Hundred and Forty-third**, called the Caverns of the Skull, discov-erd by Bursa the Bloodlettr. Iteicd. ered by Bursa the Bloodletter. Interdicted. A goddess named Kali rules this place of perpetual blood sacrifice and suffering. According to the Berevor the Wanderer. the Caverns are home to eyewings, fetch. fireshadows, hordes of fanatical petitioners, and bloodthirsty, four-armed xorn. The petitioners constantly slaughter one another and are reborn. Likewise, the layer's caverns are constantly being created and destroyed, and only the goddess knows what tunnels will seal up next. Visitors are grist for the altars. The Caverns' gates out are hidden near the most powerful proxies, and can only be activated by slaying the proxy. It is supposed that many travelers must have found their way into this layer before any found a way out.

All good brethren of the Fraternity of Order mourn the unfortunate disintegration of Orrota of the Steady Hand, Scribe Extraordinary and Arcane, whose wisdom and fearsome gaze drew the truth from travelers for these many long years. The duty of continuing the Lexicon of the Abyss now falls to Archimandrite Saki, Keeper of the Factol's Seal, Scribe to the Court of the Lady of Pain and your humble servant.

![Decorative Border with Skulls and Figures](page_252_image_2_v2.jpg)

# ALTERATIONS IN THE ABYSS

Magic poses special problems for any addle-cove visiting the Abyss, for its corruption affects the arcane as well as the physical realm. The notes below provide restricted information for the eyes of members of the Fraternity of Order only.

The effects of the large and extremely useful school of alteration magic often goes awry in the Abyss. According to the theories of the few sages familiar with the problem, alterations open a particularly strong channel to the corrupting influence of the Abyss. Both living and inanimate targets can be corrupted.

The changes to the magics in the Abyss fall into three broad categories. The investigations to date have revealed the laws governing the most common magics, listed below. Further investigation should reveal the results of Abyssal corruption on the magics of the archons and obscure spells of the Prime. We also hope to find a way to negate or bypass some of the most dangerous effects through increased understanding of the underlying laws and magical philosophy of the tanar'ri.

### LESSER TRANSFORMATIONS AND LEAST CORRUPTIONS

Effects that produce fairly minor changes in the subject's properties, but not its form, cause slight corruptions that are inconvenient at worst. The spell works as intended, but the effect goes slightly awry. Dancing lights might create several glowing, skeletal bats, a huge bolt and padlock might appear on a heid portal, a levitating or flying character might sprout wings at the neck, shoulders, or ankles.

After questioning travelers in the Broken Reach on the Plain of Infinite Portals, the best available information lists the following spells as mildly corrupted.

### LESSER CORRUPTIONS

Effects that produce dramatic changes in the subject's properties cause spectacular and potentially dangerous corruptions. The spell works, but has unintended side effects, though most are not permanent. Affect normal fires might create a spray of lava, burning hands might change the caster's hands into tiny dragon's heads, Hornung's surge suppressor might unleash a particularly nasty wild surge, a wizard eye might also report what it sees to the lord of the layer, and teleport might force the recipient to make a system shock roll (the recipient literally ceases to exist in one place and reappears in another).

### MAIOR AND INALTERABLE CORRUPTIONS

Spells that alter the subject's physical form cause extensive corruptions, even permanent ones. Living recipients change race or species and nonliving target become foul and useless. (Clerical alterations from the sphere of Law also fall into this category.) The side effects are more lethal and may affect creatures other than the caster.

Examples include the caster of a fire shield becoming immolated in flame as if he were a balor, stoneskin might petrify the recipient, claws of the umber hulk becomes a permanent spell that prevents further spellcasting, a metamorphosed liquid could be animated by a water weird, a massmorph creating an orchard of viper trees, or a strength of one spell literally melding the recipients into a single gibbering creature for the duration of the spell.

# SHRA'KT'LOR

*To my brethren in the Harmonium:*

As requested by our most conscientious Factol Sarin, I journeyed to the anarchy that is the plane of Limbo. Though its utter lawlessness and disorder threatened to overwhelm my senses, I carried out our factol's orders and now present my report to you, my brothers and sisters in the Harmonium's lawful fellowship.

I speak of the githzerai race, and the works they have built and maintained in the crashing tumult of raw chaos. Though by nature and inclination they claim to follow the tenets of chaos, after close examination of the layout of their cities and the daily habits of their lives I have concluded that the githzerai have some lawful and quite ordered beliefs.

The githzerai are a walking contradiction in terms. They are constantly at war with their racial enemies and kin, the githyanki, and yet they have built great cities in the midst of chaos and do not allow the slightest bit of chaos to intrude. I traveled to their two great cities, and observed them both.

Shra'kt'lor is as ordered and pleasant a place as could possibly be found in the unruly reaches of the Chaos side of the Great Ring. It is the githzerai's largest city and military center, and drills, patrols, and other such exercises proceed with pleasing regularity. I was allowed within the walls only for brief periods, and that only because the letters I carried from our factol proclaimed my identity. Shra'kt'lor is an austere place with seven rings of high, thick, granite walls and massive iron gatehouses. Quarters inside the city are rather cramped, and reflect the race's grim outlook. Foreigners are usually relegated to tents outside the main gates, where they cannot work mischief on the city itself or spy out its defenses. Inside the city, order is kept by companies of the god-king's army on a rotating basis. Outside the city, the Foreign Brigade—a band of mercenaries in the pay of the Great Githzerai—keeps an eye on things. If the foreign quarter gets too rowdy, these mercenaries forfeit a portion of their pay, so they watch the city of tents fairly closely.

I found that many of these guardsmen are not above taking a bribe now and again.

The Floating City, their "religious" center, shows more of chaos' influence. Though the githzerai have no priests, they revere their god-king, and under his careful eye they study the magical arts. It is a place of tall spires connected by bare stone walkways, the whole covered by a sorcerous glow. Its streets are dim, narrow, and winding, and the people pass through them in silence. Foreigners are allowed access to all the city except the central Tower of Luminescence, which houses the "god-king" and his aides. Thievery is common. Foreigners are dogged by dark, silent figures everywhere they go, and as likely as not end up leaving the city lacking the possessions they entered it with. I later discovered that these were representatives of an organization known as Thieves' Justice. This group serves double duty as a city guard and a training school for thieves. Rather than patrol the streets visibly, as guardsmen in normal cities do, these thieves maintain order by stalking the streets and fining troublemakers secretly (picking their pockets).

The Floating City is also the seat of the Anarch's Guild, though members are found throughout Limbo. Those who have earned the trust of the githzerai can get the highest level of anarch training here.

The githzerai follow the tenets and dictates of their "god-king" fervently, and accept no other leader. While I could gain no audience with either this leader or his most important advisors, it seems that he constantly exhorts his race to be on guard against the githyanki and the attacks that are expected at any moment. While no githzerai I spoke with could recall an actual githyanki attack upon either of the strongholds, all were quite unyielding in the belief that such an attack was simply a matter of time. "They're waiting for our guard to fall," claimed one elder, "and then they'll wipe us out. Why're you asking so many questions, anyway?" I assured him that I was simply interested in his people's history, and deemed it best to depart before he grew more agitated.

That encounter was by no means extraordinary; the githzerai wear suspicion like a second skin. Everywhere I was seen as an intruder at worst and an annoyance at best; I was constantly watched and measured as a possible spy of the githyanki. They insisted I be accompanied by a guide at all times while within the cities.

It is my suggestion that these people, tainted by the stain of Chaos as they are, may prove unexpected and useful agents to the Harmonium's will. To simply dismiss them as enemies of lawful order would be a waste of potential resources. Much further examination and deliberation is needful before any course of action is decided upon. I may only offer my observations and comments, as is my duty.

It is my rightful place to be,
[signature: Imara of the Harmonium]

# TAKE THE PLUNGE INTO:

# PLANES OF CHAOS

The infinite depths of the Abyss.

The wild passions of Arborea.

The immeasurable randomness of Limbo.

The howling madness of Pandemonium.

The glorious battlefields of Ysgard.

## iNside yOu'll find:

* The Book of Chaos, a 128-page guide for the Dungeon Master to the places, creatures and special conditions of the five Chaos planes.

* The Travelogue, a 48-page player's guide to these planes, profusely illustrated with full-color maps and illustrations.

* Chaos Adventures, a 32-page adventure book containing 3 adventure outlines for each plane — 15 adventures in all!

* Monstrous Supplement, a 32-page booklet detailing 15 new monsters, including new tanar'ri, the inhabitants of Yggdrasil, and the ever-changing creatures of Limbo.

* Five fully detailed, poster-size maps of the realms of Chaos.
